## 8. Carboxyphenyl Substituted Pyrazinoporphyrazines as MOF Linkers

Carboxyphenyl substituted pyrazinoporphyrazines function as promising linkers for the construction of Metal-Organic Frameworks. These macroheterocycles provide structural connectivity and stability within the framework. Their application extends to the development of advanced porous materials.

## 9. Catalytic Behavior of Ti Doping on Mg Clusters

Ti doping on Mg_n clusters exhibits specific catalytic behavior for hydrogen storage. The MgO supported TiMg_n systems also demonstrate potential in this application. Computational studies analyze the interaction mechanisms and storage capacity enhancements.

## 10. Cavitand-Based Nanocapsule for Drug Delivery

Cavitand-based nanocapsules act as smart and highly effective vehicles for drug delivery. They are specifically utilized for the transport of the anti-cancer drug 5-fluorouracil. This structure enhances targeting efficiency and therapeutic outcomes.

## 11. Cellulose Acetate Membrane Cation Selectivity

Cellulose acetate membranes demonstrate exceptional selectivity for monovalent cations over divalent cations. This property is crucial for separation processes involving ionic mixtures. The mechanism relies on the membrane's specific interaction with different cation types.

Charge transfer mechanisms boost the adsorption and activation of methane. This process occurs specifically on three-coordinated metal sites. The enhancement is attributed to electronic interactions between the metal sites and the methane molecule.

## 72. A new alkaline earthide with large nonlinear optical response

A new alkaline earthide compound has been identified as exhibiting a large nonlinear optical response. Alkaline earthides are clusters where an excess electron is trapped by alkaline earth metal cations through complexation. This electronic structure contributes significantly to the enhanced nonlinear optical properties. Theoretical calculations confirm the substantial magnitude of the response in this material.

## 73. Ligand Defect-Induced Active Sites in Ni-MOF-74 for Efficient Photocatalytic CO2 Reduction to CO

Ligand defects in Ni-MOF-74 can create active sites that enhance photocatalytic performance. The presence of these defects facilitates the adsorption and activation of CO2 molecules. Nickel sites coordinated with defective ligands show improved efficiency in reducing CO2 to CO. This defect engineering strategy offers a pathway to optimize metal-organic framework photocatalysts.

## 74. Ligand effect on Ru-centered species toward methane activation

The ligand environment significantly influences the reactivity of Ruthenium-centered species in methane activation. Specific ligand modifications can alter the electronic structure of the Ru center, affecting its interaction with methane C-H bonds. Studies on these effects help in designing more efficient catalysts for methane functionalization. The choice of ligand determines the energy barrier and selectivity of the activation process.

Optimizing the ligand structure enables the efficient separation of Americium (III) and Europium (III) in n-octanol under acidic conditions. The structural design of the ligand enhances its selectivity and resistance to acid degradation. This separation process is critical for the management of nuclear waste and the recycling of lanthanides. Acid resistance ensures the stability of the extracting agent during the separation process.

Engineering linker aggregation in Thermally Activated Delayed Fluorescence (TADF) materials allows for the tuning of carrier balance. Controlling the aggregation state of molecular linkers influences the charge transport properties within the organic LED. Optimizing carrier balance leads to higher efficiency and extended operational lifetime of the device. This approach addresses key challenges in the stability and performance of TADF-based OLEDs.

Liquid-liquid phase equilibrium data are essential for designing separation processes for pyridine-water azeotropic systems. ## 78. Lithium selective adsorption mechanism and properties of dibenzoylmethane-trioctylphosphine oxide impregnated resin

Dibenzoylmethane-trioctylphosphine oxide impregnated resins exhibit selective adsorption properties for lithium ions. The mechanism involves specific coordination interactions between the lithium ion and the functional groups of the extractant. This resin is characterized by its high capacity and selectivity for lithium over other alkali metals. Such materials are promising for lithium recovery from brine solutions.

## 79. Localized State-Induced Enhanced Emission in Perovskite Nanocrystals

Localized states within perovskite nanocrystals can induce enhanced emission properties. These states often arise from surface defects or specific lattice distortions. The confinement of charge carriers in these localized states influences the radiative recombination rate. Understanding this mechanism aids in the development of high-efficiency perovskite-based optoelectronic devices.

## 80. Long lifetimes white afterglow in slightly crosslinked polymer systems

Slightly crosslinked polymer systems are capable of exhibiting white afterglow with long lifetimes. The crosslinking structure restricts molecular motion, thereby reducing non-radiative decay. Phosphorescence from different chromophores contributes to the white emission. The stability of the crosslinked network supports persistent luminescence under ambient conditions.

The luminescent behavior of Zn(II) and Mn(II) halide derivatives of specific phosphaphenanthrene ligands has been investigated. Magnetic multi-walled carbon nanotubes functionalized with Lewis acid-base imprinted sites enable efficient recovery of Nickel (II). Non-covalent π-π interactions in molecular foldamers can manipulate single-molecule exciplex Thermally Activated Delayed Fluorescence (TADF). This strategy enables the precise control of luminescence properties in single-molecule systems.

Systematic analysis reveals trends in how specific functional groups modify the frontier molecular orbitals. ## 88. MD simulation and weak interaction analysis of evaporation phenomenon in modified BMI liquid system

Molecular Dynamics (MD) simulations are used to study the evaporation phenomenon in modified Bismaleimide (BMI) liquid systems. The mechanism and enantioselectivity of QUINOX-catalyzed asymmetric allylations of aromatic aldehydes have been elucidated. ## 90. Mechanism and Origins of Enantioselectivity in the Nickel-catalyzed Asymmetric Synthesis of Silicon-Stereogenic Benzosiloles

The mechanism underlying the enantioselectivity in Nickel-catalyzed synthesis of silicon-stereogenic benzosiloles has been investigated. The formation of the silicon stereocenter is controlled by the chiral ligand on the Nickel catalyst. Computational analysis identifies the steric and electronic factors determining the face selectivity. This research provides insights into the catalytic cycles for creating silicon chirality.

## 91. Mechanism for lithium extraction with ether and ester oxygens in armed crown ethers

The mechanism of lithium extraction using armed crown ethers involves coordination with ether and ester oxygens. The "arms" on the crown ether structure provide additional binding sites for the lithium ion. This multidentate coordination enhances the selectivity and stability constant for lithium extraction. The specific arrangement of oxygen atoms is crucial for efficient ion separation.

## 92. Mechanism of deep eutectic solvent on coal spontaneous combustion

Deep eutectic solvents (DES) can inhibit the spontaneous combustion of coal through specific chemical mechanisms. The solvent penetrates the coal structure and interacts with active functional groups responsible for oxidation. This interaction reduces the reactivity of coal with oxygen at low temperatures. The application of DES offers a preventive method for coal mine safety.

Lithium adsorption by AlLi-Layered Double Hydroxide (LDH) occurs via the Al-O octahedral ring structure. ## 94. Mechanism of thermal oxidation into volatile compounds from (E)-4-decenal-A density functional theory study

Density Functional Theory (DFT) studies reveal the mechanism of thermal oxidation of (E)-4-decenal into volatile compounds. The oxidation pathways involve the cleavage of C-H and C-C bonds at high temperatures. The formation of specific volatile products is determined by the stability of the resulting radicals. This theoretical approach helps in understanding the flavor and off-flavor formation in lipid oxidation.

## 95. Mechanism Study on Synthesis of Biobased Polycarbonate from Diphenyl Carbonate and Isosorbide with Metal-Free Catalysts by Identifying Reactivity of endo-OH and exo-OH

The synthesis of biobased polycarbonate from diphenyl carbonate and isosorbide involves the differential reactivity of endo-OH and exo-OH groups. ## 96. Mechanism, Chemoselectivity, and Stereoselectivity of an NHC-Catalyzed Reaction of Aldehydes and Hydrazones

N-Heterocyclic Carbene (NHC) catalysis governs the mechanism, chemoselectivity, and stereoselectivity of reactions between aldehydes and hydrazones. The NHC catalyst forms a reactive intermediate with the aldehyde, which then reacts with the hydrazone. The steric and electronic properties of the catalyst determine the stereochemical outcome. Understanding these factors allows for the precise control of product formation.

## 97. Mechanisms for the separation of Li+ and Mg2+ from salt lake brines using TBP and TOP systems

The separation of Lithium (Li+) and Magnesium (Mg2+) ions from salt lake brines utilizes Tributyl Phosphate (TBP) and Trioctyl Phosphine Oxide (TOP) systems. The extraction mechanism relies on the solvation differences of the two cations in the organic phase. TBP and TOP act as extractants that selectively coordinate with Li+ over Mg2+. Optimizing the solvent ratio maximizes the separation factor.

## 98. Mechanisms of acid generation from ionic photoacid generators for extreme ultraviolet and electron beam lithography

Ionic photoacid generators produce acid upon exposure to extreme ultraviolet (EUV) or electron beam (e-beam) radiation in lithography. The mechanism involves the radiolytic cleavage of the anion or cation to release a proton. The efficiency of acid generation determines the sensitivity of the resist material. Understanding these mechanisms is critical for advancing nanofabrication techniques.

## 99. Mechanistic exploration of polytetrafluoroethylene thermal plasma gasification through multiscale simulation coupled with experimental validation

The gasification of polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE) in thermal plasma is explored through multiscale simulation coupled with experimental validation. This combined approach elucidates the complex mechanisms of PTFE thermal decomposition.

## 100. Mechanistic Insight into Lewis Acid-Catalyzed Cycloaddition of Bicyclo[1.1.0]butanes with Ketene

Lewis acid catalysts promote the cycloaddition reaction between bicyclo[1.1.0]butanes and ketene. The mechanism involves the activation of the ketene by the Lewis acid, increasing its electrophilicity. The strained ring of the bicyclobutane acts as a nucleophile in the reaction. This insight allows for the synthesis of complex cyclobutane-containing structures.

## 101. Mechanistic insight into the degradation of sulfadiazine by electro-Fenton system-Role of different reactive species

The degradation of sulfadiazine in an electro-Fenton system is driven by various reactive species. Hydroxyl radicals and other oxidants play distinct roles in the decomposition pathway. ---

## 288. [3 + 2] Cycloaddition Mechanism

The mechanism, regio- and stereo selectivity, and solvent effects of [3 + 2] cycloaddition reactions involving N-benzyl fluoro nitrone and electron-deficient alkynes are revealed.
- The study provides a detailed theoretical analysis of the factors governing the reaction outcome.

---

## 289. CO2 and Ionic Liquid Interactions

The non-covalent interactions existing between CO2 and succinimide-based ionic liquids are revealed via DFT exploration.
- Quantum chemical calculations identify the specific interaction sites and energies involved in CO2 capture.

---

## 290. Sulfide and Nano-Alumina in Electrolytes

The specific role of sulfide and nano-alumina in composite solid-state electrolytes is revealed for performance-reinforced ether-nitrile copolymers.
- These additives enhance the ionic conductivity and mechanical strength of the polymer electrolyte.

---

## 291. Iron-Promoted Hydrogenation Selectivity

A reversal of regioselectivity is observed in Iron-promoted hydrogenation and hydrohalogenation of gem-Difluoroalkenes.
- The iron catalyst directs the addition to a different position compared to standard reactions, altering product distribution.

---

## 292. Crown Ether Membrane for Radionuclides

A reverse osmosis membrane decorated with crown ethers is developed for enhanced radionuclide sieving.
- The crown ether moieties act as selective binding sites to improve the separation efficiency of specific radionuclides.

---

## 293. Hetero-Halogen Electrolyte Cathode

A reversible multielectron transfer I-/IO3- cathode is enabled by a hetero-halogen electrolyte for high-energy-density aqueous batteries.
- This system leverages the multi-electron redox chemistry of iodine species to increase energy storage capacity.

---

## 294. Ratiometric Fluorescence Probe for HClO-H2S

A reversible ratiometric fluorescence probe for the detection of HClO and H2S is designed based on an excited state intramolecular proton transfer (ESIPT) mechanism.
- The probe utilizes ESIPT to shift emission wavelengths upon reaction with the target analytes.

---

## 295. AIE Mechanofluorochromism

Reversible red-blue-shifted mechanofluorochromic properties are found in indole-based difluoroboron compounds with aggregation-induced emission.
- Mechanical force induces a change in the molecular packing, leading to a shift in fluorescence color.

---

## 296. C6H62+ and C6(CH3)62+ Bonding

The bonding of the pentagonal-pyramidal C6H62+ and C6(CH3)62+ dications is revisited.
- Advanced theoretical analyses are applied to confirm the aromaticity and bonding interactions within these cationic structures.

---

## 297. Quasi-Aromaticity in Clusters

The quasi-aromaticity in polynuclear metal chalcogenide clusters and their derivative “cluster-assembly” crystalline structures is revisited.
- The concept of electron delocalization is used to explain the stability of these complex cluster architectures.

---

## 298. Iridium(III) Complexes for Electroluminescence

Rigidity-enhanced narrowband Iridium(III) complexes with finely-optimized emission spectra achieve efficient pure-red electroluminescence.
- Increasing molecular rigidity reduces non-radiative decay, improving the color purity and efficiency of the emitted light.

---

## 299. Lewis Acid Base Anchor Atoms

The role of Lewis acid base anchor atoms is crucial in catalyst regeneration.
- These anchor sites facilitate the binding and release of reactants, essential for the catalytic cycle turnover.

---

## 300. Dirhodium Catalyzed Functionalization

Running Wild through Dirhodium tetracarboxylate-catalyzed combined C-H(C)-functionalization-Cope rearrangement landscapes is explored.
- The study maps out the potential energy surfaces to understand the selectivity of this tandem reaction.

---

## 301. Safe Electrolyte for Batteries

A safe electrolyte is developed for long-cycling alkali-ion batteries.
- The formulation aims to reduce flammability and side reactions while maintaining ionic conductivity over extended cycling.

---

## 302. Salt Hydrates Co-Amorphous Systems

Salt hydrates serve as a source to form co-amorphous systems when prepared in the absence of water, analyzed via hydrogen bond analysis.
- The residual water in salt hydrates facilitates the amorphization process and stabilizes the co-amorphous matrix.

---

## 303. Salvirrane Sesquiterpene Derivatives

Salvirrane A-F are identified as six undescribed nordrimane sesquiterpene derivatives from Salvia castanea Diels f. tomentosa.
- Their structures are elucidated and cytotoxic activities are evaluated in this study.

---

## 304. Dimethoxybenzenesulfonate Crystals Properties

SC-XRD structural characterization provides insights into key electronic and non-linear optical properties of Dimethoxybenzenesulfonate crystals.
- X-ray diffraction data reveals the crystal packing responsible for the material's optical behavior.

---

## 305. Thio-Carbohydrazide Schiff Bases

Schiff bases based on thio-carbohydrazide are synthesized and characterized, followed by DFT, antimicrobial, DNA interactions, and cytotoxicity studies.
- The research establishes structure-activity relationships for these potential bioactive compounds.

---

## 306. Schwesinger Bases Stabilization

Schwesinger bases (Phosphazene bases) are stabilized by multiple counterpoise hyperconjugative interactions.
- Theoretical analysis demonstrates how electron delocalization contributes to the exceptional basicity and stability of these bases.

---

## 307. XY1b Organic Dyes Screening

Novel XY1b-based organic dyes are screened by modifying electron-assisted acceptors for dye-sensitised solar cells.
- Computational screening identifies promising molecular modifications to improve light harvesting and efficiency.

---

## 308. Deep Eutectic Solvents Screening

Green and environmentally friendly deep eutectic solvents are screened, and the molecular mechanism and process are studied.
- The research evaluates solvent performance and sustainability for specific chemical applications.

---

## 309. Scutellarin Antitumor Effects

Scutellarin activates IDH1 to exert antitumor effects in hepatocellular carcinoma progression.
- Activation of isocitrate dehydrogenase 1 (IDH1) by Scutellarin influences cancer cell metabolism and growth.

---

## 310. COF for Pyrethroid Detection

A sea-urchin-like covalent organic framework serves as a solid-phase microextraction fiber coating for sensitive detection of trace pyrethroid insecticides in water.
- The unique morphology and surface area of the COF enhance the extraction efficiency of the insecticides.

---

## 311. Copper Borozene Complexes

Stable copper borozene complexes in CuB7- and CuB8- are searched for using computational methods.
- The study investigates the electronic structure and stability of these novel boron-rich clusters.

---

## 312. SEI Dead Li in Solid-State Batteries

SEI dead Li is addressed to turn capacity loss into a gain for high-performance anode-free solid-state lithium batteries.
- Strategies are developed to mitigate the detrimental effects of solid electrolyte interphase formation on lithium metal.

---

## 313. UiO-66 Adsorption Selectivity

Selective adsorption and separation of salicylic acid and 4-hydroxyisophthalic acid from industry-grade 4-hydroxybenzoic acid are achieved on UiO-66.
- The metal-organic framework pore size and chemistry differentiate between these structurally similar acids.

---

## 314. Aluminum Oxide Cluster Adsorption

Selective organic dye adsorption properties are investigated for an aluminum oxide cluster.
- The cluster exhibits preferential binding capacity for specific dye molecules.

---

## 315. Photocatalytic Dehydrogenation on g-C3N4

Selective photocatalytic dehydrogenation of formic acid occurs on graphite carbon nitride with dual-sited cobalt and platinum cocatalysts.
- The dual cocatalysts facilitate the efficient cleavage of C-H bonds and hydrogen evolution.

---

## 316. Iodide Extraction by Chalcogen Bonding

Selective recognition and extraction of iodide from pure water are achieved by a tripodal selenoimidazol(ium) based chalcogen bonding receptor.
- The receptor utilizes selenium-based interactions to selectively capture iodide anions.

---

## 317. Rare Earth Recovery from Ammonium Sulfate

Selective recovery of low-concentration light rare earth ions from a high-concentration ammonium sulfate system is achieved by coordination ability.
- The process exploits the differing coordination strengths to separate valuable rare earth elements.

---

## 318. Polyurethane Microplastics Staining

Selective visual staining of polyurethane microplastics is enabled by a novel colorimetric and near-infrared (NIR) fluorescent dye.
- The dye specifically interacts with polyurethane, allowing for the detection and identification of these microplastics.

---

## 319. TADF Emitters Orientation

Guest-host contacts are selectively controlled to improve the horizontal orientation of thermally activated delayed fluorescence (TADF) emitters.
- Optimizing the molecular orientation within the host matrix enhances the light outcoupling efficiency of OLED devices.

---

## 320. Photodynamic-to-Photothermal Switch

A self-adaptive photodynamic-to-photothermal switch is developed for smart antitumor photoimmunotherapy.
- The system adapts its treatment modality based on the tumor microenvironment or external triggers.

---

## 321. DNA-Amino Acid Amphiphiles Materials

Self-assembled G-fold DNA-amino acid amphiphiles-based oxidase-mimetic materials exhibit drug-degrading and photoswitchable capabilities.
- The biohybrid material combines enzymatic activity with light-responsive properties for therapeutic applications.

---

## 322. Surface Active Ionic Liquids and Cellulase

Self-assembly of choline-based surface active ionic liquids leads to a concentration-dependent enhancement in the enzymatic activity of cellulase in an aqueous medium.
- The ionic liquid aggregates create a favorable microenvironment for the enzyme's catalytic function.

---

## 323. Metal-Polyphenolic Network on Biomass

Self-assembly of a metal-polyphenolic network on biomass enhances organic contaminant capturing from water with a high cost-to-benefit ratio.
- The coated biomass material acts as an efficient and economical adsorbent for water purification.

---

## 324. Polymers for NIR-II Imaging

Semiconducting polymers based on asymmetric thiadiazoloquinoxaline are used for augmented in vivo NIR-II photoacoustic imaging.
- The polymer's optical properties allow for deep-tissue imaging with high resolution and contrast.

---

## 325. Deep Eutectic Solvents for Indium Separation

Separation of indium and tin from ITO powders is performed using short-chain dicarboxylic acid-ChCl deep eutectic solvents.
- The solvent system effectively dissolves and separates the metal components from indium tin oxide waste.

---

## 326. SERS Sensor with Mixed Clusters

A SERS-based sensor uses subnanometric copper–silver mixed clusters Ag(8–n)Cun (n = 0–8) for pyridine detection.
- The bimetallic clusters provide enhanced surface-enhanced Raman scattering signals for the analyte.

---

## 327. Photoresponsive Molecular Crystals

Shape dependence is observed in photoresponsive molecular crystals composed of naphthyl acrylic acid stimulated by solid-state [2 + 2] photocycloaddition.
- The crystal morphology influences the efficiency and outcome of the solid-state photoreaction.

---

## 328. Dye Molecules in Dyed Silk

The composition and fading mechanisms of dye molecules in Phellodendron amurense Rupr. dyed silk are elucidated.
- Analysis identifies the chemical degradation pathways responsible for the color fading in the historical textile.

---

## 329. Hydrogen and Halogen Bond Cooperativity

The strength, nature, and cooperativity-anti-cooperativity between intermolecular H…N hydrogen and Cl…C halogen bonds are investigated.
- The study quantifies how these two types of non-covalent interactions influence each other when present in a system.

---

## 330. Side-Chain Engineering in QD-LEDs

Side-chain engineering in small-molecule hole transport layer materials improves the performance of high-performance QD-LEDs.
- Modifying the side chains optimizes film morphology and charge injection/transport properties.

---

## 331. Reverse Saturable Absorption Enhancement

Significant enhancement of reverse saturable absorption ability is achieved via Pt(II) incorporation into the conjugated backbone.
- The heavy metal atom promotes intersystem crossing, enhancing the nonlinear optical performance.

---

## 332. Room-Temperature Phosphorescence Enhancement

Significant room-temperature phosphorescence enhancement is induced by matrix complexes.
- The rigid matrix environment restricts molecular motion, reducing non-radiative decay and boosting phosphorescence.

---

## 333. Heat-Resistant Energetic Materials

Heat-resistant energetic materials based on [5,6,5] tricyclic fused-ring skeletons are prepared simply and their energetic performance is evaluated.
- The fused-ring structure contributes to high thermal stability and energy density.

---

## 334. Ni-MOF-74 for CH4-N2 Separation

A simulation study investigates functional group-modified Ni-MOF-74 for CH4-N2 adsorption separation.
- Modifications to the organic linkers tune the pore environment for improved selectivity between methane and nitrogen.

---

## 335. CO2 Radical Anions for Antibiotics Degradation

Simultaneous degradation, dehalogenation, and detoxification of halogenated antibiotics are achieved by carbon dioxide radical anions.
- The radical anion acts as a strong reducing agent to break down halogenated organic contaminants.

---

## 336. Difenoconazole Stability and Enrichment

Simultaneous enhancement of thermal stability and preferred isomer enrichment is achieved in Difenoconazole.
- The formulation or processing method stabilizes the compound and isolates the most active isomer.

---

## 337. Singlet Oxygen for Contaminant Mineralization

Singlet oxygen promotes high mineralization of pharmaceutically active contaminants via persulfate-assisted mAg3PO4@g-C3N4 photocatalysis.
- The generation of singlet oxygen drives the oxidation of organic pollutants into harmless inorganic compounds.

---

## 338. NIR-II Fluorogenic Dye for Bioimaging

Site-specific albumin tagging with an NIR-II fluorogenic dye enables high-performance and super-stable bioimaging.
- Covalent attachment to albumin prolongs circulation time and enhances fluorescence for in vivo imaging.

---

## 339. Zinc-Doped Boron Clusters

Size effects and electronic properties are studied for zinc-doped boron clusters ZnBn- (n = 1–15).
- The cluster size dictates the electronic structure and stability of these doped boron clusters.

---

## 340. Silicon Clusters Optical Responses

Size-dependent linear and nonlinear optical responses are investigated for silicon clusters.
- The optical properties of the clusters evolve as the number of silicon atoms changes.

---

## 341. Asphaltene Dispersants in Softeners

Softeners doped with asphaltene dispersants act in synergy with resins, revealing molecular mechanisms underlying asphalt rejuvenation.
- The combination restores the viscoelastic properties of aged asphalt by dissolving and dispersing asphaltenes.

---

## 342. Solid-State Effect on TADF Luminescence

The solid-state effect on the luminescence mechanism of thermally activated delayed fluorescence with aggregation-induced emission is studied.
- Molecular packing in the solid state influences the emission efficiency and lifetime of TADF materials.

---

## 343. BOPHY Dyes Solid-State Emission

Solid-state emissive meso-Aryl-Alkyl-substituted and heteroatom-mixed BOPHY dyes are synthesized.
- Structural modifications enable these dyes to maintain high fluorescence quantum yields in the solid state.

---

## 344. Solute-Solvent Interaction of 9,9-Dihydroxynanoic Acid

Solute-solvent interaction of 9,9-dihydroxynanoic acid is studied by DFT, IR, Raman, UV, MEP, quantum parameters, and docking studies.
- A multi-technique approach reveals the structural and energetic aspects of its interaction with the solvent.

---

## 345. Investigations of 6-Chloro-2-(4-Aminophenyl)-1H-Benzimidazole

Solvation model, vibrational analysis, electronic level, non-covalent interactions, and molecular docking investigations are performed on 6-Chloro-2-(4-Aminophenyl)-1H-Benzimidazole.
- Comprehensive computational and theoretical methods characterize the molecule's properties and potential biological interactions.

---

## 346. Solvent Effects on 1-Benzyl-Indole-3-Carbinol

Solvent effects on the structural, spectroscopic, electronic properties, NCI-RDG analysis, molecular docking, and molecular dynamics studies of 1-benzyl-indole-3-carbinol are evaluated.
- The solvent environment significantly alters the conformational stability and intermolecular interactions of the molecule.

---

## 347. Solvent Role in Sulpha Drug Derivative

The solvent role in molecular structure level, structural and topology investigations, molecular docking, and antimicrobial activity studies is examined for a sulpha drug derivative.
- Different solvents affect the molecular conformation and its interaction with biological targets.

---

## 348. Solvent-Dependent Foldamer Folding

Solvent-dependent folding behavior is observed in a helix-forming aryl-triazole foldamer.
- The foldamer adopts specific helical conformations in response to the polarity and nature of the solvent.

---

## 349. Chiral Self-Assembly of POSS

Solvent-mediated chiral self-assembly of nanosized π-conjugated polyhedral oligomeric silsesquioxane leads to full-spectrum luminescence.
- The assembly process, directed by solvent chirality, creates optically active nanostructures.

---

## 350. Molecular Interaction of Antipsychotic Drug

Molecular interaction, antibonding & nonbonding, thermodynamic properties, and MD simulation analysis of 1-(4-Aminophenyl)-4-(4-methoxyphenyl) piperazine (antipsychotic drug) with solvents are studied.
- The research characterizes the physicochemical behavior of the drug in various solvent environments.

---

## 351. Micelle Formation Regulation in Extraction

The problem of the 'third phase' in the extraction process is solved by regulating the formation of micelles.
- Controlling micellization prevents the formation of a problematic interphase during solvent extraction.

---

## 352. Co(II)-Schiff Base Complexes Anticancer Activity

Some Co(II)-Schiff base complexes are identified as promising anticancer agents through DFT and molecular docking studies.
- The theoretical studies support the potential of these complexes to interact with biological targets relevant to cancer.

---

## 353. Germanium(IV) Spectrophotometric Study

A spectrophotometric and DFT study investigates Germanium(IV) interaction with 6,7-dihydroxy-2,4-diphenyl-1-benzopyran-1-ium bromide in solutions.
- The combined approach determines the stoichiometry and stability constants of the complex formed.

---

## 354. Favipiravir-Guanine Complex

Spectroscopic characterization and quantum chemical calculations are performed on the Favipiravir-Guanine biomolecular complex.
- The study explores the non-covalent interactions between the antiviral drug Favipiravir and the nucleobase Guanine.

---

## 355. Carbon Monoxide Activation by Chromium

Spectroscopic characterization is performed for carbon monoxide activation by neutral chromium carbides.
- Experimental data provides evidence for the binding and activation of CO on the carbide surface.

---

## 356. Chromium Tricarbonyl Excited States

Spectroscopic characterization is conducted for highly excited neutral chromium tricarbonyl.
- The study probes the electronic states and relaxation dynamics of the metal carbonyl complex.

---

## 357. 5-Methoxy-1H-Benzimidazole Complex Studies

Spectroscopic characterization, DFT calculations, antimicrobial activity, and molecular docking studies are conducted for 5-methoxy-1H-benzo[d]imidazole and its Ag(I) complex.
- The research correlates the structural features of the complex with its biological activity.

---

## 358. Biimidazole Derivative Studies

Spectroscopic characterization, DFT, antimicrobial activity, and molecular docking studies are performed on 4,5-bis[(E)-2-phenylethenyl]-1H,1'H-2,2'-biimidazole.
- The conjugated system and its metal interactions are analyzed for potential pharmacological applications.

---

## 359. Pyrozole-Thiazole RDG and Docking

Spectroscopic characterizations, RDG, and docking study are performed on a specific pyrozole-thiazole derivative.
- Non-covalent interaction analysis and docking simulations elucidate the molecule's binding affinity.

---

## 360. Nicotinamide Computational Analysis

Spectroscopic, quantum computational, topological, Fukui functions, and molecular docking analysis are performed on Nicotinamide as a potential anti-cancer molecule using the DFT method.
- These analyses predict the reactivity and potential mechanism of action of Nicotinamide.

---

## 361. Spin Effect in Fe-Catalyzed Hydrosilylation

Spin effect influences redox acceleration and regioselectivity in Fe-catalyzed alkyne hydrosilylation.
- The spin state of the iron catalyst plays a critical role in determining the reaction rate and product distribution.

---

## 362. Sr@C14 Carbon Ring Cluster

Sr@C14 is identified as a stable Sr-centered monocyclic carbon ring cluster.
- Theoretical calculations confirm the thermodynamic stability and electronic structure of this endohedral metallofullerene analog.

---

## 363. Ca@C60 Oligomers and Polymers

Stability and electronic properties are investigated for 1D and 2D Ca@C60 oligomers and polymers.
- The study explores how connecting Ca@C60 units affects the material's overall electronic behavior.

---

## 364. Mixed Rare-Earth Metallofullerenes

Stability and electronic properties are studied for mixed rare-earth tri-metallofullerenes YxDy3–x@C80 (x = 1 or 2).
- The variation in metal composition within the carbon cage tunes the electronic characteristics of the molecule.

---

## 365. Metal Doped C9 Ring Stabilization

Stabilization of ring structures of C9 is achieved with the doping of a metal atom in an ab initio study.
- The metal atom incorporation provides additional electronic stabilization to the monocyclic carbon ring.

---

## 366. CH3I and CH2I2 State-Selective Dissociation

State-selective dissociative double ionization of CH3I and CH2I2 via I 4d core-hole states is studied by multi-electron–ion coincidence spectroscopy.
- The experiment maps out the decay pathways of the core-excited molecules leading to fragmentation.

---

## 367. Ionic Liquid for Mercury Capture

An electrophilic N center in ionic liquid is steered for efficient oxidative capture of elemental mercury.
- The ionic liquid acts as a reactive medium to oxidize and trap mercury vapor.

---

## 368. Chiral Transport in Porous Media

Stereoselective transport of 2-aryl propionic acid enantiomers in porous media is subjected to chiral organic acids.
- The chiral selector in the porous matrix differentially transports the enantiomers based on their handedness.

---

## 369. Luminescence Steric Control in Trityl Radicals

Steric control of luminescence is observed in phenyl-substituted trityl radicals.
- Bulky substituents restrict molecular rotation, enhancing the luminescence quantum yield of the radicals.

---

## 370. Supramolecular Complexes Stoichiometry

Stoichiometry validation of supramolecular complexes with a hydrocarbon cage host is performed by van ’t Hoff analyses.
- Thermodynamic data derived from temperature-dependent studies confirm the host-guest binding ratio.

---

## 371. Noncovalent Bonds Strengthening

Strengthening of noncovalent bonds is caused by internal deformations.
- Mechanical strain or conformational changes within a molecule can enhance the strength of its weak interactions.

---

## 372. Isohexide-Based Copolycarbonates

Strong, tough, and heat-resistant isohexide-based copolycarbonates are developed as an ecologically safe alternative for bisphenol-A polycarbonate.
- Bio-based isohexide monomers provide sustainable materials with high performance.

---

## 373. Hydride Copper Cages

Structural alterations are described for copper cages containing hydrides coordinated to the central Pd(0).
- The presence of hydride ligands modifies the geometry and electronic structure of the copper-palladium cluster.

---

## 374. Magnesium Cluster Anions Doped with Beryllium

Structural and electronic properties of medium-sized magnesium cluster anions doped with two beryllium atoms are investigated.
- Doping with beryllium alters the electronic configuration and stability of the magnesium clusters.

---

## 375. Lignin Fractionation by DES

Structural characterization is performed for lignin fractionated by acidic deep eutectic solvents and lignin nanoparticles fabricated from Camellia Oleifera shell.
- The deep eutectic solvent selectively extracts lignin fractions suitable for nanoparticle production.

---

## 376. Benzofuran Isomers Elucidation

Structural confirmation of position isomers 2-(2-methylaminoprolyl)benzofuran and 5-(2-methylaminopropyl)benzofuran is achieved via combined mass spectrometric and computational study.
- The integration of experimental and computational data distinguishes between the subtle structural differences of the isomers.

---

## 377. Florfenicol Inclusion Complex

Structural elucidation and release enhancement of the Florfenicol-β-cyclodextrin inclusion complex are demonstrated.
- Complexation with β-cyclodextrin improves the solubility and release profile of the drug Florfenicol.

---

## 378. Pyridine Intermolecular Interactions

Structural elucidation and various computational studies are performed for quantitative investigation of intermolecular interactions in pyridine-2,6-dicarboxylic acid and its di-hydrate.
- The research quantifies the hydrogen bonding and stacking interactions present in the crystal structures.

---

## 379. Cerium Doped Germanium Nanocluster

Structural evolution and electronic properties of cerium doped germanium anionic nanocluster CeGen- (n = 5–17) are investigated.
- The doping of cerium significantly influences the growth pattern and electronic structure of the germanium clusters.

---

## 380. X–PCIC Acceptor Modification

Structural modification of an A-C-A configured X–PCIC acceptor molecule achieves efficient photovoltaic properties with low energy loss in organic solar cells.
- Tuning the acceptor structure optimizes the energy level alignment and charge transfer in the solar cell.

---

## 381. Phenothiazine D–π–A Frameworks

Structural, electrical, optical, and DFT studies of phenothiazine-based D–π–A frameworks are conducted for dye-sensitized solar cell applications.
- The donor-π-acceptor design enhances light absorption and electron injection efficiency.

---

## 382. Salamo-Type Ligand Complex

Structural, fluorescent, and theoretical studies of a flexible salamo-type ligand and its uncommon tetranuclear chloride-bridged nickel(II) complex are presented.
- The tetranuclear structure exhibits unique photophysical properties due to the specific arrangement of metal centers.

---

## 383. Antihistamine Agent Investigation

Structural, spectral analysis, reactivity, topological, and pharmacological investigations are conducted on an antihistamine agent.
- Comprehensive profiling aids in understanding the drug's mechanism and potential efficacy.

---

## 384. 2-Hydroxychalcones Characterization

Structural, spectroscopical, electronic, non-linear optical characterization, and antioxidant activity of 2-hydroxychalcones para-derivatives are studied.
- The para-substitution pattern is correlated with the antioxidant capacity and optical properties of the chalcones.

---

## 385. Methyl-2-(4-Isobutylphenyl)propanoate Analysis

Structural, topological, vibrational, and electronic analysis, and ADMET study of Methyl-2-(4-isobutylphenyl)propanoate are performed.
- The in silico ADMET study predicts the pharmacokinetics and toxicity of the molecule (related to Ibuprofen).

---

## 386. Diarylpropanes ECD Spectra

Structurally diverse 1,2-diarylpropanes from the fruit of Crataegus pinnatifida are investigated, revealing mirror-image ECD spectra with the same absolute configurations.
- This phenomenon highlights the complexity of correlating electronic circular dichroism with stereochemistry in similar compounds.

---

## 387. Cationic Lipids with Tryptophan Assembly

Structure and interaction in 2D organized assembles of cationic lipids with tryptophan are studied.
- The cationic lipids and amino acid form organized monolayers driven by electrostatic and hydrophobic interactions.

---

## 388. Cyclocarbon and Pentazole Composites

Structure and properties of new composites composed of the all-carbon molecule cyclocarbon (and cyclo[18]carbon) and the polynitrogen molecule pentazole are investigated.
- The study explores the high-energy density and stability of these exotic molecular composites.

---

## 389. Metal Ion Effects on Cellulose Fibers

Structure and properties variations of regenerated cellulose fibers are induced by metal ion impurities.
- The presence of metal ions alters the hydrogen bonding network and crystallinity of the cellulose fibers.

---

## 390. Dihydropyridine Computational Investigations

Structure elucidation, DFT, and IRI computational investigations are performed on a novel dihydropyridine derivative.
- The theoretical methods visualize and quantify the intramolecular and intermolecular interactions within the molecule.

---

## 391. Inverse Sandwich Complexes

Structure, stability, and reactivity of inverse sandwich complexes M-Be3-M and M-Zn3-M (M = ?) are investigated.
- These complexes feature metal atoms sandwiching a trigonal planar cluster of beryllium or zinc.

---

## 392. Structure-dependent H-abstraction kinetics on heterorings

Heterorings exhibit structure-dependent kinetics during hydrogen atom abstraction reactions. The rate of these abstraction processes varies significantly depending on the specific ring structure. Computational studies often utilize density functional theory to analyze these kinetic variations.

---

## 393. Structures and bonding characteristics of KCl(H2O)n clusters (n = 1–10)

KCl(H2O)n clusters (where n ranges from 1 to 10) possess distinct structures and bonding characteristics analyzed via density functional theory. The geometric configuration of the clusters evolves as the number of water molecules increases. The interaction between the potassium ion, chloride ion, and water molecules dictates the stability of these hydrated clusters.

---

## 394. Structures and hydrogen bonds of -SO3H functionalized acid ionic liquids

-SO3H functionalized acid ionic liquids form specific structures stabilized by hydrogen bonds. The presence of the sulfonic acid group (-SO3H) significantly influences the hydrogen bonding network within the liquid. These structural features determine the physicochemical properties of the ionic liquids.

---

## 395. Structures of Ni-doped Bn (n = 1–13) clusters

Ni-doped boron clusters (Bn, n = 1–13) have been investigated through computational studies. The introduction of a nickel atom alters the geometric and electronic structures of the pure boron clusters. Stability often peaks at specific cluster sizes due to electronic shell closures or geometric symmetry.

---

## 396. Catalytic activity of methylene bridged dicationic -SO3H functionalized imidazolium phosphomolybdate hybrids

Methylene bridged dicationic -SO3H functionalized imidazolium phosphomolybdate hybrids act as catalysts for one-pot sequential synthesis. The combination of acidic sulfonic groups and phosphomolybdate anions contributes to high catalytic activity. These hybrid materials facilitate multi-step reactions in a single reactor vessel.

---

## 397. Interactions in Piperazine Benzoate and their effect on biological properties

Piperazine Benzoate involves various intermolecular interactions that influence its biological properties. The nature of these interactions, such as hydrogen bonding or van der Waals forces, affects the compound's bioactivity and stability. Computational analysis helps elucidate the relationship between molecular interactions and biological efficacy.

---

## 398. Molecular mechanism of phenolics extraction from alkaline wastewater

The separation of phenolics from alkaline wastewater involves synergistic extraction mechanisms. Molecular docking and dynamics simulations reveal how extractants interact with phenolic compounds. The efficiency of extraction depends on the specific molecular interactions and the solvent environment.

---

## 399. p-tolylpiperidin-4-one as an anti-Parkinson agent

New p-tolylpiperidin-4-one derivatives have been synthesized and evaluated as anti-Parkinson agents. Studies include spectral analysis, X-ray crystallography, and *in silico* investigations to understand their electronic properties. Intermolecular interactions play a crucial role in the drug's potential efficacy.

---

## 400. Supramolecular organic frameworks for hydrogen purification

Supramolecular organic frameworks are studied for hydrogen purification via molecular dynamics simulations. These frameworks possess specific pore structures that selectively interact with hydrogen molecules. The separation efficiency is determined by the diffusive behavior of gases through the supramolecular channels.

---

## 401. Catalytic pyrolysis mechanism of guaiacol over seaweed-derived carbon catalyst

The pyrolysis mechanism of guaiacol over seaweed-derived carbon catalysts is investigated using density functional theory and machine learning. The carbon catalyst's surface properties influence the reaction pathways and product distribution. Machine learning models assist in predicting catalytic performance and reaction kinetics.

---

## 402. Mechanism of phenolic compound removal using hydrophobic deep eutectic solvents

The removal of phenolic compounds from wastewater is achieved using hydrophobic deep eutectic solvents. Experimental and microscopic mechanisms elucidate the extraction efficiency. The hydrophobic nature of the solvent enhances the partitioning of phenolic compounds from the aqueous phase.

---

## 403. Microscopic mechanism of stepwise charge injection in co-sensitive DSSCs

Stepwise charge injection in co-sensitive dye-sensitized solar cells (DSSCs) is analyzed within the framework of D-PI-A dyes and chlorophyll. The mechanism involves sequential electron transfer steps from the dye to the semiconductor. Molecular design of the dye influences the efficiency of charge injection and co-sensitization.

---

## 404. Gas phase reaction mechanism of HCN and H2O

The reaction mechanism between HCN and H2O in the gas phase is studied based on density functional theory. The interaction leads to the formation of various products through specific transition states. Computational results provide insights into the reaction energetics and pathway feasibility.

---

## 405. Inherent characteristics of asphalt regenerating agents

Asphalt regenerating agents possess inherent characteristics that affect their performance in pavement recycling. These characteristics include chemical composition and viscosity. Understanding these properties is essential for optimizing the regeneration of aged asphalt.

---

## 406. Multiscale structures and digestibility of cassava starch-fatty acid complexes

Cassava starch complexes with medium-chain fatty acids exhibit specific multiscale structures affecting digestibility. Molecular simulation techniques reveal how fatty acids interact with starch chains. The formation of V-type amylose complexes influences the enzymatic resistance of the starch.

---

## 407. Separation of acetonitrile and methanol azeotrope using deep eutectic solvents

The separation of the acetonitrile and methanol azeotropic system utilizes tetraethylammonium chloride-based deep eutectic solvents. These solvents break the azeotrope by altering the relative volatility of the components. The separation efficiency depends on the solvent composition and physical properties.

---

## 408. Separation of methanol and dimethyl carbonate azeotrope using deep eutectic solvents

Deep eutectic solvents are employed to separate the methanol and dimethyl carbonate azeotropic system. The solvents selectively interact with one component to facilitate separation. This method offers a potentially greener alternative to traditional extractive distillation processes.

---

## 409. Solvent effect and coordination characteristics of Cu(II) salamo-salen-salamo-hybrid complexes

Cu(II) salamo-salen-salamo-hybrid complexes exhibit specific solvent effects and coordination characteristics. The hybrid ligand structure influences the geometry and electronic state of the copper center. Solvent polarity can impact the stability and spectral properties of these complexes.

---

## 410. Direct current properties of ultraviolet crosslinked EPDM rubber

Ultraviolet crosslinked EPDM rubber displays distinct direct current properties. Crosslinking alters the dielectric behavior and conductivity of the material. The degree of crosslinking correlates with the insulation performance and electrical stability.

---

## 411. Interaction of tannins and salivary proteins affecting wine aroma volatility

The interaction between tannins and salivary proteins influences the volatility of wine aroma compounds. Protein-tannin binding can reduce the perceived aroma by retaining volatile molecules. This interaction affects the sensory perception of wine astringency and flavor.

---

## 412. Mechanism of extractive distillation separation of cyclohexane-ethanol azeotrope

The separation of the cyclohexane-ethanol azeotrope via extractive distillation involves ionic liquids as separating agents. The ionic liquid disrupts the azeotropic behavior by increasing the relative volatility of ethanol. The mechanism is explained by the molecular interactions between the solvent and the azeotrope components.

---

## 413. Microscopic Mechanism of the Grain Refinement of Al-Ti-B Master Alloy

The grain refinement mechanism in Al-Ti-B master alloys operates on a microscopic scale. The presence of TiB2 particles and Al3Ti phases facilitates heterogeneous nucleation. This results in a finer grain structure within the aluminum matrix.

---

## 414. Saltiness-enhancing mechanism of chicken-derived umami peptides

Chicken-derived umami peptides enhance saltiness by interacting with the transmembrane channel-like protein 4 (TMC4). Sensory evaluation combined with molecular docking confirms this interaction. The peptides bind to specific sites on the protein, modulating sodium ion perception.

---

## 415. Synthesis mechanism of pyrazoles via [3+2] cycloaddition

Pyrazoles are synthesized via [3+2] cycloaddition reactions of diazocarbonyl compounds with enones lacking leaving groups. The reaction proceeds through a concerted or stepwise mechanism depending on the substituents. The absence of leaving groups in the enone simplifies the reaction pathway.

---

## 416. Treatment of coking wastewater by Fenton-like system based on natural pyrite

A Fenton-like system utilizing natural pyrite is effective for treating coking wastewater biochemical effluent. Pyrite catalyzes the generation of hydroxyl radicals from hydrogen peroxide. This advanced oxidation process degrades organic pollutants in the wastewater.

---

## 417. Viscosity mechanism of caffeine crystallization solutions

The viscosity mechanism in caffeine crystallization solutions is influenced by solute-solvent interactions. Changes in concentration and temperature affect the solution's flow behavior. Understanding viscosity is crucial for controlling the crystallization process and crystal quality.

---

## 418. Substituent effects on the coordination of benzo-21-crown-7 and dibenzo-21-crown-7 with cesium

Substituents on benzo-21-crown-7 and dibenzo-21-crown-7 affect their coordination ability with cesium ions. Electronic and steric effects of the substituents alter the binding affinity and selectivity. These modifications are crucial for optimizing crown ethers in cesium separation applications.

---

## 419. Substituent effects on emission quantum efficiency in pyrene-based luminogens

Substituents impact the emission quantum efficiency of pyrene-based luminogens featuring two-photon absorption. Theoretical and experimental studies show how structural changes affect photophysical properties. Optimizing substituents can enhance the performance of these materials in imaging and sensing.

---

## 420. Substituent effects on the ESIPT process of 2′-aminochalcone derivatives

The ESIPT (Excited-State Intramolecular Proton Transfer) process in 2′-aminochalcone derivatives is influenced by substituent effects. Substituents can alter the energy barrier for proton transfer, affecting emission wavelengths. Potential applications in the materials transport field are linked to these photophysical changes.

---

## 421. Driving force for quinoidal isoindigo-based diradicaloids

Quinoidal isoindigo-based diradicaloids possess a sufficient driving force for diradical character. The extent of diradical character is tunable through molecular design. This property is essential for applications in organic electronics and spintronics.

---

## 422. Sulfation of birch wood xylan with sulfamic acid

Birch wood xylan can be sulfated using sulfamic acid in the presence of activators. Both experimental and theoretical methods investigate the reaction mechanism and product structure. This process modifies the xylan for use in various industrial applications.

---

## 423. Sulfur and chloride co-doped g-C3N4 in photocatalytic peroxymonosulfate activation

Sulfur and chloride co-doped g-C3N4 enhances the photocatalytic activation of peroxymonosulfate. Co-doping improves the charge separation and surface reactivity of the catalyst. This material shows enhanced performance in the removal of acetaminophen.

---

## 424. Super-Defects in Superatomic Crystals

Superatomic crystals can exhibit structures characterized as "super-defects." These defects arise from the assembly of superatoms in non-ideal configurations. The presence of super-defects can significantly alter the electronic and magnetic properties of the material.

---

## 425. Superalkali-alkaline earthide ion pairs with large NLO responses

Ion pairs consisting of superalkalis and alkaline earthides, denoted as δ+(AM-HMHC)-AM’δ-, exhibit large non-linear optical (NLO) responses. The specific combination of alkali (Li, Na, K) and alkaline earth (Be, Mg, Ca) cations/anions dictates the NLO activity. These systems are of interest for advanced optical materials.

---

## 426. Superatomic Ag58 nanoclusters with [MS4@Ag12]2+ kernel

Superatomic Ag58 nanoclusters incorporate a [MS4@Ag12]2+ (M = Mo or W) kernel. These nanoclusters display aggregation-induced emission properties. The structural integrity of the kernel is crucial for the observed photophysical behavior.

---

## 427. Superatomic Aromaticity in Cyclic Superatomic Molecules

Cyclic superatomic molecules, such as ligand-protected penta-icosahedral [M@Au11]5- clusters, exhibit superatomic aromaticity. The electronic delocalization follows the Hückel rule adapted for superatomic orbitals. This property contributes to the stability of the clusters.

---

## 428. Dielectric energy storage performance of high-temperature film capacitors

High-temperature film capacitors achieve superior dielectric energy storage performance through molecular structure design. Tailoring the polymer chains enhances thermal stability and dielectric strength. This allows for reliable operation under high-temperature conditions.

---

## 429. Supported Ionic Liquid Membrane with Polyamide Armor for CO2 Separation

A supported ionic liquid membrane featuring a highly-permeable polyamide armor is created via *in situ* interfacial polymerization. This design ensures durable and efficient CO2 separation. The polyamide layer protects the ionic liquid while maintaining high gas permeability.

---

## 430. Supramolecular Assemblies with unusual non-covalent contacts

Supramolecular assemblies in Fumarato and Succinato bridged polymers of Co(II) and Ni(II) feature unusual N(nitrile)???π(fum) and Nitrile-Nitrile contacts. These non-covalent interactions drive the formation of specific polymeric architectures. The nature of the bridging ligands dictates the topology of the resulting network.

---

## 431. Supramolecular clumps of μ2-1,3-acetate bridges

Supramolecular clumps are formed via μ2-1,3-acetate bridges in Cd(II)-Salen complexes. The acetate groups link multiple metal centers to form larger aggregates. This bridging mode influences the structural dimensionality and stability of the complex.

---

## 432. Supramolecular compounds from [{CpMo(CO)2}2(μ,η2-AsSb)] and metal salts

Supramolecular compounds are assembled from the heteroleptic tetrahedral complex [{CpMo(CO)2}2(μ,η2-AsSb)] and various metal salts. The AsSb bridge provides a unique structural motif for further assembly. Coordination with metal salts leads to extended supramolecular structures.

---

## 433. Supramolecular Interaction between Berberine Hydrochloride and Baicalin

Berberine Hydrochloride and Baicalin engage in supramolecular interactions in aqueous solution. These interactions likely involve stacking or hydrogen bonding, affecting the solubility and bioavailability of the drugs. Studying this complex provides insights into drug-drug interactions.

---

## 434. Supramolecularly Dimeric Assemble of Planar Cu13 Clusters

Planar Cu13 clusters form supramolecularly dimeric assemblies controlled by the length of diphosphine spacers. The spacer length dictates the distance and orientation between the clusters. This control allows for the tuning of the intermolecular coupling properties.

---

## 435. Surface engineered carbon quantum dots for photocatalytic H2O2 production

Surface engineered carbon quantum dots are utilized for efficient photocatalytic hydrogen peroxide production. Modifying the surface chemistry enhances charge separation and catalytic sites. This leads to higher yield and selectivity in H2O2 synthesis.

---

## 436. Surface modification of TM decorated Pt-doped CQDs as nonenzymatic sensors

Transition metal (Mn, Fe, Co) decorated Pt-doped carbon quantum dots (Pt@CQDs) are surface modified for use as nonenzymatic sensors. The bimetallic doping and surface engineering enhance electrocatalytic activity. These nanostructures are effective for detecting specific analytes without enzymes.

---

## 437. Switchable deep eutectic solvent for phenoxyacetic acid herbicides microextraction

A switchable deep eutectic solvent acts as a green media for liquid-phase microextraction of phenoxyacetic acid herbicides. The solvent properties can be altered to facilitate extraction and recovery. This method is applicable to water and food matrices.

---

## 438. Symmetry-breaking charge separation in a rigid nonconjugated trimer

Symmetry-breaking charge separation occurs in a null-excitonic 3-dimensional rigid nonconjugated trimer. The rigid structure prevents exciton delocalization, promoting charge separation. This mechanism is relevant for improving the efficiency of organic photovoltaic devices.

---

## 439. Synergetic effects of hydrogen bonding in XC5H3HC?=?Y···HO···H2O2 complexes

Complexes of the type XC5H3HC?=?Y···HO···H2O2 (X = N, P, As, Sb; Y = O, S, NH) exhibit synergetic effects of inter- and intramolecular hydrogen bonding. The interplay between these bonds stabilizes specific conformations. The nature of X and Y influences the strength and geometry of the interactions.

---

## 440. Synergic Delocalized-Conjugate and Electron-Deficient Effect in photocatalytic coupling

The synergic effect of delocalized-conjugate and electron-deficient characteristics promotes photocatalytic coupling of H2 evolution with benzyl alcohol oxidation. Mesoporous channels in the catalyst facilitate mass transport. This dual-functional mechanism enhances overall photocatalytic efficiency.

---

## 441. Synergistic catalytic biomass-H2O gasification for H2 production

Synergistic catalytic biomass-H2O gasification is studied for H2 production and biochar etching via experimental and DFT methods. The catalyst promotes the reforming reactions to maximize hydrogen yield. The process also involves the structural transformation of the biochar byproduct.

---

## 442. Synergistic effect of carbon dopants on water splitting

Carbon dopants on graphitic carbon nitride have a synergistic effect on photoinduced water splitting. Doping introduces mid-gap states that enhance visible light absorption. This modification improves the efficiency of hydrogen and oxygen evolution.

---

## 443. Synergistic Effect of Small Molecular Organic Matter and Functional Groups in Coal

Small molecular organic matter and functional groups in coal have a synergistic effect on methane adsorption. The combined interactions increase the adsorption capacity compared to individual components. This understanding aids in modeling gas storage in coal seams.

---

## 444. Synergistic Intramolecular Non-Covalent Interactions in TADF emitters

Robust pure-blue TADF (Thermally Activated Delayed Fluorescence) emitters are enabled by synergistic intramolecular non-covalent interactions. These interactions rigidify the molecular structure and suppress non-radiative decay. This results in improved efficiency and color purity for blue OLEDs.

---

## 445. Synergistic mechanism of ternary green corrosion inhibitors

A ternary green corrosion inhibitor system for N80 steel in HCl solution works via a synergistic mechanism. Vanillin is encapsulated and transported through micelles formed by ionic liquids. This delivery system enhances the protective layer formation on the steel surface.

---

## 446. Synergistic modulation of hydrogen bond network and pH buffering

The synergistic modulation of hydrogen bond network reconstruction and electrolyte pH buffering enables highly reversible Zn anodes. This regulation prevents dendrite formation and side reactions. Stable cycling performance is achieved through this dual approach.

---

## 447. Synergistic Potential of Zidovudine Drug Encapsulating in g-C3N4 Nanosheet

Encapsulating Zidovudine in g-C3N4 Nanosheets shows synergistic potential for enhanced HIV-AIDS therapy. Quantum mechanical investigations reveal the drug-nanosheet interactions. This carrier system may improve drug delivery and efficacy.

---

## 448. Synergistic regulation of chloride anion recognition

Chloride anion recognition is regulated synergistically using a triple-functional sites receptor with two different cationic effectors. The binding sites cooperate to enhance selectivity and affinity for chloride. This multi-component strategy allows for precise anion sensing.

---

## 449. Synergistic silver doping and N vacancy in carbon nitride

Synergistic silver doping and nitrogen vacancy in carbon nitride promote photocatalytic performances for pollutant oxidation and hydrogen production. The defects and dopants act as co-catalysts and active sites. This combination significantly boosts the quantum yield of the reactions.

---

## 450. Synergistic strategies of metallization and redox coupling in porphyrin COF

Metallization and redox coupling strategies synergistically enhance the quantum yield and recyclability of porphyrin covalent organic frameworks (COFs). Incorporating metals improves charge separation and transport. Redox coupling facilitates the catalytic cycle and regeneration of the material.

---

## 451. Synergy between electron donor and steric hindrance in Alkylated-Piperazine absorbents

Alkylated-Piperazine absorbents utilize the synergy between electron donor ability and steric hindrance for efficient CO2 capture. The electron donor strength increases CO2 affinity, while steric hindrance prevents excessive regeneration energy. This balance optimizes the absorption-desorption cycle.

---

## 452. Syntheses and energetic properties of cyclo-pentazolate salts

Cyclo-pentazolate salts [NHn(CH3)4-n]+N5- (n = 0～3) are synthesized and their energetic properties evaluated. The cation composition influences the stability and detonation performance of the N5- anion. These compounds are of interest as high-energy density materials.

---

## 453. Synthesis and analysis of a novel phospho-nitrile flame retardant

A novel phospho-nitrile flame retardant is synthesized and analyzed for cotton fabric application. The compound combines phosphorus and nitrogen elements to enhance flame retardancy. Analysis confirms its thermal stability and effectiveness on textile substrates.

---

## 454. Synthesis and Characterization of an All-Germanium Analogue of Cyclobutane-1,3-diyl

An all-germanium analogue of cyclobutane-1,3-diyl has been successfully synthesized and characterized. The molecule features a Ge=Ge double bond or a diradicaloid structure analogous to its carbon counterpart. Spectroscopic data confirm the bonding nature of the germanium atoms.

---

## 455. Synthesis and Characterization of Pyrazole-Triazolone Compound

A Pyrazole-Triazolone compound is synthesized and characterized for its exceptional heat resistance. The fused heterocyclic structure contributes to high thermal stability. This property makes it suitable for high-temperature applications.

---

## 456. Synthesis and dyeing performance of heterocyclic azo disperse dyes

Novel heterocyclic azo disperse dyes are synthesized for dyeing polylactic acid fiber. The dyes exhibit good affinity and color fastness on the synthetic fiber. The molecular structure influences the dyeing performance and spectral properties.

---

## 457. Synthesis and energy storage performances of fluorinated polyimides

Fluorinated polyimides are synthesized and evaluated for high-temperature energy storage performances. Fluorination increases the dielectric constant and reduces dielectric loss. These polymers are suitable for advanced film capacitors.

---

## 458. Synthesis and mechanism of quaternary ammonium salts as copper levelers

Quaternary ammonium salts based on porphyrin are synthesized as high-performance copper levelers. The mechanism involves adsorption onto the copper surface to modulate deposition rates. These additives improve the uniformity of copper plating.

---

## 459. Synthesis and photophysical properties of dinuclear NHC copper(I) complexes

Dinuclear N-heterocyclic carbene (NHC) copper(I) complexes are synthesized and their photophysical properties studied. These complexes exhibit photoluminescence suitable for light-emitting diodes. They also find applications in anti-counterfeiting inks.

---

## 460. Synthesis and property of an O-doped aromatic belt

An O-doped aromatic belt is synthesized and its properties investigated. The doping of oxygen atoms into the belt structure alters its electronic conjugation. This modification affects the optical and electronic behavior of the system.

---

## 461. Synthesis and Reactions of 3-Amino-5,6-Dimethyl-l,2,4-Triazine

3-Amino-5,6-Dimethyl-l,2,4-Triazine derivatives undergo synthesis and reaction studies. DFT studies, ADME assays, and molecular docking are performed to evaluate the new derivatives. These analyses assess the potential biological activity of the triazine compounds.

---

## 462. Synthesis and Spectroscopic Studies of Rhenium Tetraarylporphyrin Complexes

Rhenium Tetraarylporphyrin complexes are synthesized and studied using spectroscopic techniques. The coordination of rhenium to the porphyrin ring influences the electronic spectrum. These complexes are relevant for catalysis and medical applications.

---

## 463. Synthesis and Study of Azido-tetrazole Behavior in Energetic Materials

The steering of Azido-tetrazole behavior is studied in Tetrazolo[1,5-c]pyrimidin-5-amine-based energetic materials. The azido-tetrazole tautomerism affects the stability and sensitivity of the material. Understanding this behavior is crucial for safe handling and performance.

---

## 464. Synthesis of (5-Iminio-4,5-dihydro-1,3,4-oxadiazol-2-yl)tetrazol-1-ide

A zwitterionic compound containing a tetrazole anion, (5-Iminio-4,5-dihydro-1,3,4-oxadiazol-2-yl)tetrazol-1-ide, is synthesized. The structure features both positive and negative charges within the same molecule. This unique arrangement influences its crystal packing and stability.

---

## 465. Synthesis of 2-amino-5-methylpyridinium tetrachloridocadmate(II)

2-amino-5-methylpyridinium tetrachloridocadmate(II) with the formula (C6H9N2)2[CdCl4] is synthesized. The compound consists of organic cations and inorganic anions. X-ray diffraction reveals the hydrogen bonding network between the ions.

---

## 466. Synthesis of a 50-electron triangular ruthenium cluster

A 50-electron triangular ruthenium cluster containing a μ3-methylidyne ligand is synthesized via Ru2 skeleton fragmentation. The cluster features a unique carbon-centered ligand bridging the ruthenium triangle. The electronic structure satisfies the 18-electron rule for transition metal clusters.

---

## 467. Synthesis of a Guanidine Salt Oligomer via Ion Exchange

A Guanidine Salt Oligomer with low metal-corrosive and high-antibacterial properties is synthesized via ion exchange. The oligomeric structure enhances its effectiveness as a biocide. The ion exchange process ensures the counterion is non-corrosive to metals.

---

## 468. Synthesis of asymmetric cationic zinc porphyrin photosensitizer

An asymmetric cationic zinc porphyrin photosensitizer containing a thiophene group is synthesized for photodynamic therapy. DFT calculations support the design of the photosensitizer for *in vitro* application. The thiophene group and cationic charge improve cellular uptake and ROS generation.

---

## 469. Synthesis of cyanated naphthalene diimide-Hydroazaacenes

Cyanated naphthalene diimide-Hydroazaacenes are synthesized as amphoteric-redox compounds with low-lying LUMOs. The cyanation and azaacene fusion enhance electron-accepting capabilities. These materials are promising for n-type organic electronics.

---

## 470. Synthesis of dimeric 1,2-benzothiazine 1,1-dioxide scaffolds

Dimeric 1,2-benzothiazine 1,1-dioxide scaffolds are synthesized and analyzed. Studies include molecular structures, Hirshfeld surface analysis, DFT, and enzyme inhibition. The dimeric structure influences the biological activity and intermolecular interactions.

---

## 471. Synthesis of High Purity 1,3-bis-(3-Sulfonic acid)propyl Imidazolium Ionic Liquids

High purity 1,3-bis-(3-Sulfonic acid)propyl Imidazolium Ionic Liquids are synthesized. The sulfonic acid groups impart high acidity and hydrophilicity. Purity is essential for consistent performance in catalytic applications.

---

## 472. Synthesis of inter-[60]fullerene conjugates with inherent chirality

Inter-[60]fullerene conjugates possessing inherent chirality are synthesized. The连接方式 between fullerene units induces chirality without traditional chiral centers. These chiral fullerene derivatives have potential in asymmetric catalysis and optoelectronics.

---

## 473. Synthesis of magnetic bifunctional monomer molecularly imprinted polymers

Magnetic bifunctional monomer molecularly imprinted polymers are synthesized for the separation of paclitaxel from Taxus cuspidata. The magnetic core allows easy recovery, while the imprinted cavities provide selectivity. This method enhances the extraction efficiency of the anti-cancer drug.

---

## 474. Synthesis of magneto-thermal biresponsive temperature-sensitive copolymer nanoemulsifier

A magneto-thermal biresponsive temperature-sensitive copolymer-inorganic hybrid is synthesized as a green nanoemulsifier. The copolymer exhibits a tunable Lower Critical Solution Temperature (LCST). The material responds to both magnetic fields and temperature changes.

---

## 475. Synthesis of new [6]helicene derivatives for OLED applications

New [6]helicene derivatives are synthesized for use in OLED applications. Experimental studies cover photophysical and chiroptical properties, supported by theoretical investigation. The helicene structure contributes to circularly polarized luminescence.

---

## 476. Synthesis of novel supramolecular selenomolybdate as anticancer agents

Novel supramolecular selenomolybdates are synthesized and investigated as anticancer agents. Experimental and DFT computational analyses reveal their mechanism of action. The polyoxometalate structure interacts with biological targets to inhibit cancer cell growth.

---

## 477. Synthesis of Phosphinoboranes via Bromine Abstraction

Phosphinoboranes are synthesized via coordination-induced bromine abstraction of an isolable phosphinyl radical with bromoboranes. This reaction mechanism involves the formation of a P-B bond through radical intermediates. The method provides a new route for main group element bonding.

---

## 478. Synthesis of Ultramicroporous Zeolitic Imidazolate Framework ZIF-8

Ultramicroporous Zeolitic Imidazolate Framework ZIF-8 is synthesized via a solid-state method using minimal deionized water. This method is efficient for producing high-surface-area ZIF-8 for greenhouse gas adsorption. The minimized solvent use makes the process greener.

---

## 479. Synthesis and biological activity of guanidino-containing neonicotinoid derivatives

Novel guanidino-containing neonicotinoid derivatives are synthesized and their biological activity evaluated. The guanidino group enhances interaction with the insect nicotinic acetylcholine receptor. Binding modes are studied to understand the insecticidal mechanism.

---

## 480. Synthesis, biological activity of 2-phenylthiazole-1,3,5-triazine derivatives

2-phenylthiazole-1,3,5-triazine derivatives are synthesized, characterized, and tested for biological activity. Studies include X-ray crystallography, DFT calculations, and molecular dynamics simulations. The fused thiazole-triazine system contributes to the bioactivity.

---

## 481. Synthesis, solvent effects on acridine-1,8-diones-proline fluorescent probe

Acridine-1,8-diones-proline based fluorescent probes are synthesized and characterized. Solvent effects significantly influence their electronic properties and non-linear optical activity. These probes are useful for sensing applications in different media.

---

## 482. Synthesis and Anticancer Potential of Pyrene-Appended Schiff Base Tin(IV) Complexes

Pyrene-Appended Schiff Base Tin(IV) Complexes are synthesized, characterized, and evaluated for anticancer potential. The pyrene moiety provides fluorescence for tracking, while the tin complex acts as the active agent. The complexes intercalate with DNA or inhibit enzymes.

---

## 483. Synthesis and computational analysis of aromatic hydrazone compounds

Aromatic hydrazone compounds are synthesized and subjected to comprehensive computational analysis. The study focuses on their electronic structure and potential tautomerism. Computational results complement experimental spectroscopic data.

---

## 484. Synthesis and electronic structure of Bioactive Vanillin based fluorescent Schiff bases

Bioactive Vanillin based fluorescent Schiff bases are synthesized and their electronic structure studied. The vanillin aldehyde group condenses with amines to form the Schiff base. The resulting compounds exhibit fluorescence useful for bioimaging.

---

## 485. Synthesis and gas adsorption properties of CTF-1 based porous polymer

A porous polymer based on the covalent triazine framework CTF-1 is synthesized and characterized. Its gas adsorption properties are tested for SO2, CO2, NO2, CH4, CO, NO, and N2. The material shows selectivity based on pore size and surface chemistry.

---

## 486. Synthesis, Characterization, and Properties of Sila-Annulated Phenanthrene Imides

Sila-Annulated Phenanthrene Imides are synthesized, characterized, and their properties studied. The replacement of carbon with silicon (sila-annulation) alters the electronic and steric properties. These compounds are of interest in materials science.

---

## 487. Synthesis and fabrication of photosensitive Schottky device of binuclear Cu(II)-Salen complex

A binuclear Cu(II)-Salen complex is synthesized and used to fabricate a photosensitive Schottky device. The complex acts as the active semiconductor layer in the device. Photoresponse characteristics are evaluated under illumination.

---

## 488. Synthesis, Crystal Structure, and Computational Investigations of (Z)-3-N-(methyl)-2-N’-(4-methoxyphenylimino)

The compound (Z)-3-N-(methyl)-2-N’-(4-methoxyphenylimino) is synthesized and analyzed. Studies include crystal structure determination, Hirshfeld surface analysis, and computational investigations. Molecular docking studies reveal potential biological targets.

---

## 489. Synthesis, crystal structure, and DFT calculations of a triazol-4-yl prop-2-en-1-one

(2e)-3-(dimethylamino)-1-[5-methyl-1-(4-nitrophenyl)-1h-1,2,3-triazol-4-yl]prop-2-en-1-one is synthesized and analyzed. Crystal structure, Hirshfeld surface analysis, energy frameworks, and DFT calculations are performed. These analyses provide insights into molecular stability and interactions.

---

## 490. Synthesis, crystal structure, and anti-microbial investigation on morpholinium adipate

Morpholinium adipate is synthesized, and its crystal structure is determined. Hirshfeld surface analysis and spectral characterization are conducted. The compound is investigated for its anti-microbial properties.

---

## 491. Synthesis, Crystal Structure, and Computational Analysis of Bis-[imidazolium] Oxalate Ethanol Solvate

Bis-[2-(3,4-dihydroxyphenyl)-4,5-diphenyl-1H-imidazol-3-ium] Oxalate Ethanol Solvate is synthesized. Crystal structure, Hirshfeld surface, and computational analysis are performed. The study highlights the supramolecular interactions in the solvate.

---

## 492. Synthesis, crystal structure, and molecular docking analysis of ethenone derivative

1-(4(tert-butyl)-4-methoxy-[1,1-biphenyl]-4-yl) ethenone is synthesized and analyzed. Quantum chemical computation and molecular docking analysis are performed. The study investigates its potential interaction with biological targets.

---

## 493. Synthesis, Crystal Structure, and Computational Analysis of Phenanthro[9,10-d]Imidazol-2-yl Phenol

2,4-Dibromo-6-(1H-Phenanthro[9,10-d]Imidazol-2-yl)Phenol is synthesized as a charge-transport system. Spectral (IR, NMR) and crystal structure analyses are conducted. Computational studies support its application in electronic devices.

---

## 494. Synthesis, mesophase behavior, and computational studies of liquid crystal dimers

Dimers composed of three-ring rod-shaped ester-imine linkage monomeric units are synthesized. Their mesophase behavior is studied using experimental and computational methods. The length and flexibility of the spacer influence the liquid crystal phases formed.

---

## 495. Synthesis and Nonlinear Optical Properties of Amaranth dye doped crystal

Amaranth dye doped L-Lysine Sulphate (ALLS) Semiorganic Crystal is synthesized. Molecular structure, experimental, and computational studies are performed. The Nonlinear Optical (NLO) properties are evaluated for potential device applications.

---

## 496. Synthesis, Photophysical and Electronic Properties of Pyrenyl-o-Carborane

A D-π-A Julolidine-like Pyrenyl-o-Carborane is synthesized and studied. Its photophysical and electronic properties are characterized. The carborane unit acts as a strong electron acceptor in the system.

---

## 497. Synthesis, Photophysical, and Optical Limiting Properties of Twistacene-Functionalized Arenes
